# Actual root support and root-space lines in algebraically closed rank one

`Langlands.GroupScheme.algClosedRankOneRootSupportLines` proves
`AlgClosedRankOneRootSupportLinesStatement.{u}` as published, for arbitrary
universe `u`. Given the algebraically closed field, the connected reductive
group, its prescribed split maximal torus, a compatible root datum and the
pairwise dependence of actual roots over `ℤ`, the proof gives support on
`{P.root r, -P.root r}` and both linear equivalences for each root index `r`.
[Statement](RankOneRootSupportLinesStatement.md)

The proof applies `algClosedRankOneRootSupportLines_of_centralCover` to the
published theorem `rankOneCentralCover`. The reduction obtains
`P.root r ∈ roots i` from the equality of the actual root set and the image of
`P.root` in `IsRootDatumOf i P`. This witnesses the nonempty root set needed
for the cover. The cover supplies compatible group and torus isogenies from
`SL₂ × C` and `D × C`, with `C` split and `D` the diagonal torus of `SL₂`.
`eq_or_eq_neg_of_sl2_centralCover` pulls the actual roots back along the
torus isogeny, uses opposition for the explicit `SL₂ × C` roots, and descends
the resulting equality through injectivity of character pullback.
[Cover reduction](IndependentRootCellReduction.md)

`RankOneCentralCover.rootLineOfCover_isogeny` uses an injective matrix entry
on an `SL₂` root space, the product projection, and the equivalence induced
by the central-isogeny differential on nonzero weight spaces. It is applied
to `P.root r` and to the negative root supplied by
`IsRootDatumOf.neg_mem_roots`. The signed support comes from the standard
cover and works with the published hypotheses on the supplied root datum.
[Root-line transfer](RankOneCentralCoverLines.md)

This is an internal deduction from published Library theorems. The cover's
local line argument uses the ordinary flag variety `G/B`. Białynicki–Birula
limits, the fixed-point classification and Hartogs extension give the
dimension bound for `G/B`. The proof applies the general curve lemmas
`HomogeneousQuotient.curveUnipotentDataOfPseudoReductive` and
`HomogeneousQuotient.unipotent_dimension_le_one` with this bound and the
proved noncontainment in `B`. The local lines yield an internal root cell,
which gives the compatible cover. [Cover proof](RankOneCentralCoverProof.md)

## Dependency audit

The list at the end of this section and the central-cover inventory it extends together name the Library declarations on which the proof depends. The Weil restriction and its point operations come from the Library's own modules `Langlands.RingTheory.WeilRestriction.PresentedAlgebra` (the Weil restriction, its universal point, representing bijection and functoriality), `Langlands.RingTheory.WeilRestriction.PointMultiplication` (its bialgebra structure and the convolution product of its points) and `Langlands.RingTheory.WeilRestriction.Units` (the points of the Weil restriction of the multiplicative group as units); further entries come from `Langlands.RingTheory.HopfAlgebra.PointGroup`, `Langlands.RingTheory.Coalgebra.TensorConvolution`, `Langlands.RingTheory.HopfAlgebra.CocommutativePoints`, `Langlands.RingTheory.Extension.Presentation.MapsOut` and `Langlands.Algebra.Polynomial.Laurent.Smooth`. The list contains only public declarations of the modules named here, not their private declarations or generated auxiliary lemmas such as `_proof_` lemmas.

A Lean check traversed the theorem's type and
value, then the type and value of every constant reached. The scan uses
`Expr.getUsedConstants` and `ConstantInfo.value? (allowOpaque := true)` for
every reached constant, with a visited set and breadth-first traversal.
It found 115,374 constants including the theorem, with zero hits among
the 25 named excluded declarations, the seven additional route declarations
below, and every declaration attributed to
`Langlands.GroupScheme.SimplyConnected.SplitCorootSpanLineReduction`.
The scan verified that all named cut declarations and the excluded module
were present in its environment. An inspection
of the theorem reported universe `u` and the
axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.

The scan checked these 32 names in `Langlands.GroupScheme`.
